Unknown outcomes block replay.\\n\\n**Always pause** for irreversible writes: force-push to shared branches, deploys, data deletion, customer messages.\\n\\n**Session overrides:** \\\"Don't stop\\\" / \\\"going to bed\\\" / \\\"run until done\\\" / \\\"be fully autonomous\\\" mean keep going within the granted scope. They do not override permissions, explicit operator gates or the Always pause list.\\n\\n**No is an acceptable answer.** Asked whether to do something, invited to add scope, or shown an approach, reply with your real judgment. Decline, push back, or say \\\"this doesn't earn its place\\\" when true. A recommendation is a judgment, not a validation. Agreement is not the default, candor over sycophancy.\\n\\n## Subagents\\n\\nUse the [poteto role prompt](../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) for any independent worker you spawn inside a playbook step, including code-writing delegates and ad-hoc helpers. /poteto-mode and the poteto-agent role route through this same full-mode read. Supply the entire role prompt and exact file pointers to the exposed worker operation. Generic routing without that prompt and read skips the style contract. Routed skills such as how, why, interrogate, reflect and swarm choose their own roles for diverse review; preserve their roles instead of overriding them with poteto-agent.\\n\\nDefaults for every worker assignment follow the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Use independent background execution only when exposed and observed. Do not claim it from a role name or flag. Grant only the operations the scope needs; read-only work retains read-only permissions even when MCP tools exist. Use file pointers rather than inlined bulk context. Record requested and observed model and effort separately under [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md).\\n\\nCode delegates tier by difficulty. Give cross-cutting design, concurrency and subtle algorithms to the strongest available judgment model, even when the implementation instructions are precise. Trivial mechanical edits use an available fast code model. Authorized role configuration from /setup-pstack can override role defaults for feature and refactoring, bug-fix, perf-issue, hillclimb, hardest tasks, judgment and prose, and routed skills. Select only supported models and efforts from current tool documentation or observed enumeration. A missing role line keeps the portable inherit-parent default. A role line of inherit-parent or auto omits explicit model selection. Unsupported configured models or efforts require a disclosed supported fallback, or blocked if that exact choice is required. The source-host named defaults are not portable availability evidence. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified.\\n\\nWithout an exposed and authorized independent-worker operation, apply the same full-mode and leaf reads sequentially only within authorized scope. Label reduced capability. Sequential roles are not independent workers, independent review, background execution or model diversity. If the playbook requires independence, leave that gate blocked rather than silently waiving it.\\n\\nA worker forbidden to spawn owns its assigned diff directly and returns it to the lead for review separation, as Feature step 4 requires. It never waits on a nested worker or replies \\\"standing by\\\". This exception does not waive the lead's required independent review. Before accepting any result, match assignment identity, exact input revision, full current head SHA, existing artifacts and observed method evidence. Reject stale results and missing artifacts under the assignment contract. Bookkeeping labels cannot satisfy these checks.\\n\\nYou own every subagent's work. Review the diff and write your own summary, don't pass through what it said. A second opinion is the same prompt against a different model. Treat agreement as supporting evidence only after checking independence, the observed models and the underlying artifacts.\\n\\n**Fresh subagents by default.** Give new work to a fresh subagent with consolidated scope, meaning the original brief, every later directive, and the prior agent's report and branch. This holds for a fix round, a follow-up, a retry, and the next queue item. Resume, message, or queue a follow-up on an existing subagent only when the new work strictly needs state that lives in that agent and is costly to move: its local checkout, its uncommitted changes, or a process it still runs, such as a dev server, a simulator, or a babysit watcher. A stop or hold order to a running agent is not reuse. A role such as a PR owner outlives its agent. Once that agent returns, a fresh agent takes the role's next round. Interrupt-chained resumes silently drop directives, so fire a fresh subagent with consolidated scope rather than trusting a \\\"done\\\" summary.\\n\\n## Throughput checkpoint\\n\\nFor nontrivial multi-step work, write these four todo items before fan-out, as required by Feature step 3. Keep an inapplicable dimension with n/a and a reason. Rewrite the checkpoint at phase boundaries and return it with the evidence.\\n\\n- Blocking first steps. Gates run before fan-out.\\n- Independent workstreams. Disjoint files, services or layers parallelize. Shared writes serialize.\\n- Shared mutable state. Default to splitting the target using the separate-before-serializing-shared-state principle leaf. Serialize only for real invariants.\\n- Smallest safe decomposition. If one worker is best, name why.\\n\\nCode-coupled work goes to one owner with the checkpoint inline. That owner fans out after the blocking phase when authorized and supported. Parent-level fan-out is for independent artifacts. Assignment records and bookkeeping do not prove workers ran. Keep decision trails in an explicit owned local output path per show-me-your-work and its log helper contract. Missing helper setup or persistence stays visible; do not invent saved logs or cross-session continuity.\\n\\n## Integrated helper and delivery bindings\\n\\nRead the selected playbook's complete adapted helper and prompt contracts before dependent execution. The 23 bundled playbook routes below and the Bugbot rubric are local instructions. Historical task deferrals in those files are not proof of absence or permission to skip a present contract. Check the actual path. An absent required contract still blocks its dependent step.\\n\\n- Decision trails use [show-me-your-work](../show-me-your-work/SKILL.md), its [template](../show-me-your-work/references/decision-log-template.tsv) and [log helper](../show-me-your-work/scripts/log.sh).\\n\\n  Invoke the helper through authorized execution with exactly `<logfile> <phase> <decision> <why> <evidence> <result>`.\\n\\n  The nonempty logfile is an explicit owned local path.\\n\\n  Tabs, newlines and carriage returns are rejected before writes, and formula-leading cells are quoted.\\n\\n  Read back the trail and retain the skill's scoped transcript audit, cross-model review and Attention requirements.\\n\\n  Append-only logging proves neither unattended execution nor recovery.\\n- Multi-phase plans use [check-plan.mjs](scripts/check-plan.mjs) as `bun --no-install <package-root>/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/check-plan.mjs <plan.md>`.\\n\\n  It takes one positional path and reads without edits.\\n\\n  Exit 2 means usage or unreadable input, 1 means format problems, and 0 means structural success.\\n\\n  Keep capability declarations, live and perf evidence, independent review and exact-head receipts.\\n\\n  It checks pstack plans only.\\n\\n  It never validates another controller's plan, implementation or host behavior.\\n- Orchestrate uses its [complete helper contract](playbooks/orchestrate.md) and the real [CLI](scripts/orch/orch.ts) and [store](scripts/orch/store.ts).\\n\\n  Bind `bun --no-install <package-root>/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/orch/orch.ts --store <owned-store>` or explicit `ORCH_STORE` outside the package tree.\\n\\n  Use full JSON for standing orders and rows.\\n\\n  Preserve `inbox drain --peek --json` and actual drain receipts with durable readback before classification. `ledger check <pr> <full-head-sha>` returns NOT-VERIFIED and exit 2 for a missing key.\\n\\n  Usage or runtime errors exit 1.\\n\\n  Ledger rows replace the same key without enforcing verifier precedence.\\n\\n  The caller owns precedence, append-only attempt history, exact SHA syntax, artifact inspection, terminal states, permissions and current-head filtering.\\n\\n  The helper never spawns, waits or wakes workers.\\n\\n  Stale locks require ownership review, and `--force` requires explicit takeover permission even for a dead holder.\\n\\n  Frontier discovery uses local git and gt in the stack owner's clone and fails on unavailable metadata or pin mismatch.\\n\\n  Do not substitute this binding for the gt-free Autopilot stack workflow.\\n\\n  Atomic rename is not a crash-durability or adversarial-concurrency guarantee.\\n- Babysit and Shipping use the GitHub-specific read-only [watcher](scripts/watch-pr/watch-pr) through their complete playbooks.\\n\\n  From the package root bind `bun --no-install skills/poteto-mode/scripts/watch-pr/watch-pr` with explicit `--owner`, `--repo`, PR selection, `--head <number=full-40-character-head>` for every watched member, `--max-polls <remaining-count>` and `--timeout <remaining-seconds>`.\\n\\n  Freeze queued order with `--queued-stack --stack-prs <bottom-to-top-list>`.\\n\\n  Connected mode uses `--stack`, mutually exclusive with queued mode. `check` adds `--status-only`.\\n\\n  Inspect NDJSON schemaVersion 1 kind, terminal and reason.\\n\\n  Exit 0 includes status-only and proves no readiness by itself.\\n\\n  Codes 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7 and 64 identify conflicts, threads, checks, timeout, merge gates, query failures and usage.\\n\\n  A changed head, missing authentication or transport, or exhausted poll-budget blocks the result.\\n\\n  Queued WAITING with reason kind merge-queue is non-terminal.\\n\\n  Babysit reports the frontier merge-ready and stops that watcher.\\n\\n  Shipping waits for observed merge under its distinct rule.\\n\\n  Carry budgets across re-arms and retain an observed stop or deadline binding for a hard wall-clock limit.\\n\\n  The helper deadline is checked between polling work.\\n\\n  Do not add a second sleep loop or treat GitHub output as Origin evidence.\\n- Cleanup uses the [audit wrapper](scripts/worktree-audit.sh) and [implementation](scripts/worktree-audit.py) through its playbook with an explicit local `--repo` and verified `--base`, plus authorized `--history-map` and `--pr-state` exports when available. The helper returns logical bytes and evidence buckets, performs no fetch or deletion, and leaves unknown facts as holds. A bucket never grants deletion permission. Recheck actual usage and ignored files before separately authorized removal.\\n\\nThe [bootstrap](scripts/bootstrap.ts), [manifest](scripts/package.json) and [lock](scripts/bun.lock) require exact commander 14.0.0, bun-types 1.3.14 and typescript 7.0.2 metadata before dependency-backed CLI execution. Bootstrap fails setup-required and never installs. Run helpers with Bun runtime auto-install disabled. Missing dependencies remain blocked. The isolated tested snapshot only supports that snapshot, not this caller, the current checkout or either host. [Helper setup](../../docs/testing/helper-setup.md) states the exact-copy validation boundary and retains historical setup evidence. It does not authorize installation or transfer a historical pass to this caller. Any future setup needs fresh authorization for an exact writable copy, registry, disabled lifecycle scripts and task-owned cache. Never install in an operative invocation or bypass bootstrap.\\n\\nDelivery reads [Opening a PR](playbooks/opening-a-pr.md) in full and retains its review, companion, commit, push, draft or ready authorization and actual-result gates. A local draft is not a created PR. An exposed built-in PR tool owns operations it covers, and a failed tool does not authorize a CLI bypass. Opening alone never starts Babysit. A read-only question stays in Investigation, with no PR; its specific exception takes precedence over the generic delivery route. Prototype and planning handoffs grant no implementation or external permission. Missing create-skill, deslop, matching controls or independent review remains an explicit blocked gate even when a reduced local artifact is useful.\\n\\n## Writing the reply\\n\\nWrite the reply clean as you draft it. A cleanup pass after drafting does not remove these patterns.\\n\\n- **Short declarative sentences.** One thought per sentence, ended with a period.\\n- **No long-dash character anywhere.** Write a file-list bullet as a sentence (\\\"`main.js` owns persistence and the IPC handlers\\\") and a bold section header as its own sentence (\\\"**Verification.** End to end via CDP\\\").\\n- **A colon as a mid-sentence connector is also out** (unslop rule 14). A colon before a list is fine.\\n- **Terse is not an excuse to drop content.** Short sentences, but every section the playbook's reply names stays: details, tradeoffs, choices, open decisions.\\n- **Frame impact for the consumer and the maintainer.** Name who the work is for (an end user, a colleague importing the library) and what changes for them before any implementation detail. Then what the next engineer who owns this code inherits. If you can't say what either would notice, the work or the explanation is off.\\n- **Never fabricate a link, citation, or transcript reference.** Link only artifacts you produced or read this session.\\n- **Every claim carries its evidence or its label in the same sentence.** Measured, inferred, or guess. A prediction or an unseen cause is a guess. Never hand the human a check you could run.\\n\\nEvery playbook ends with a reply written this way. Link to the actual PR you read or created when authorized. Never claim a draft is a created PR. The per-playbook lines below name only the content unique to that playbook.\\n\\n## Comments\\n\\nComments follow the same rule as the reply. Write them clean as you go. Keep a comment only for a non-obvious *why* the code can't show. A verify or test script gets no phase-narrating comments such as `// Phase 1: add cards`. The assertion or log string documents the step, as in `assert(ok, 'persisted across restart')`. This applies to every file you produce, including the delegate's diff.\\n\\n## Playbooks\\n\\nOpen an available todo list, or an authorized local Markdown checklist if no todo operation exists. Record that reduced tool binding. Its first items are the matched playbook's steps, copied in verbatim, before any task-specific todos. A step you choose not to do stays in the list with a one-line `skip: <reason>`. Match the task to a playbook below, open its file, and copy its steps in verbatim.\\n\\nA large or cross-cutting effort (a migration across many call sites, an ambitious multi-part change), or work the user steps away from to trust later, routes to the **figure-it-out** skill even when a narrower playbook like Feature fits. Use **figure-it-out** whenever no bundled playbook fits. It designs a bespoke, rigorous playbook for the task. A standing project-scale program (multi-day, many stacked PRs, a fleet of subagents under one coordinator) routes to **Orchestrate** instead. figure-it-out designs one bespoke run, orchestrate runs the program.\\n\\n- **Investigation.** Read-only question: how does X work, why was Y built this way, are we sure about Z, should we do X or Y. `playbooks/investigation.md`.\\n- **Bug fix.** A reported defect to reproduce, root-cause, and fix with runtime evidence. `playbooks/bug-fix.md`.\\n- **Perf issue.** A measured slowness to trace and improve against a baseline. `playbooks/perf-issue.md`.\\n- **Hillclimb.** Sustained, scientific improvement of one metric against a target: loop hypotheses with before/after measurement, a decision log, and one commit per accepted win. Distinct from Perf issue, which is a one-off fix. `playbooks/hillclimb.md`.\\n- **Runtime forensics.** Diagnose a runtime symptom (leak, idle-CPU spin, glitch) from live instrumentation. The deliverable is a diagnosis, not a fix. `playbooks/runtime-forensics.md`.\\n- **Trace forensics.** Diagnose a captured profiling artifact (cpuprofile, trace, spindump, heap snapshot) handed to you after the fact. The deliverable is a diagnosis, not a fix. `playbooks/trace-forensics.md`.\\n- **Feature.** New or changed behavior, built from a named data shape. `playbooks/feature.md`.\\n- **Refactoring.** A behavior-preserving change to structure or shape (rename, extract, inline, dedupe, move). `playbooks/refactoring.md`.\\n- **Prototype.** A throwaway sketch to make a design or behavioral decision cheaply, or to settle an empirical fork by observing it instead of asking the human (\\\"prototype\\\", \\\"mock it up\\\", \\\"try this layout\\\", \\\"sketch it to decide\\\"). `playbooks/prototype.md`.\\n- **Visual parity.** Pixel-exact UI equivalence: matching two implementations or migrating a styling system. `playbooks/visual-parity.md`.\\n- **Authoring or modifying a skill.** Writing or editing a SKILL.md. `playbooks/authoring-a-skill.md`.\\n- **Eval.** Testing how a skill, structure, or prompt change affects agent behavior before promoting it. `playbooks/eval.md`.\\n- **Babysit.** Driving a PR or a stack to merge-ready: conflicts, review threads, CI. `playbooks/babysit.md`.\\n- **Shipping.** The half after Babysit. Independently verifying a green stack, then landing the contiguous verified run bottom-up through `gh` by default or Origin when its CLI is available. `playbooks/shipping.md`.\\n- **Autonomous run.** A long task to drive to completion without stopping (\\\"run until done\\\", \\\"/loop until X\\\"). `playbooks/autonomous-run.md`.\\n- **Orchestrate.** A standing project handed to one coordinator chat: multi-day, many stacked PRs, dozens to hundreds of subagents, minimal human turns (\\\"run this whole project\\\", \\\"own this migration until it lands\\\"). Distinct from Autonomous run, which drives one task to a predicate. Work one agent could finish inside the session's budget routes there, not here, however program-shaped the phrasing sounds. `playbooks/orchestrate.md`.\\n- **Autopilot-full.** A queue of independent PRs run to merged with full autonomy. One owner per PR carries build through merge, and the root swarm-verifies each PR before its owner merges (\\\"autopilot this queue\\\", \\\"full autopilot\\\", one-owner-per-PR programs). `playbooks/autopilot-full.md`.\\n- **Autopilot-stack.** A queue of changes built and verified with full autonomy, delivered as one linear reviewed base-branch stack the operator lands (\\\"autopilot-stack\\\", \\\"stack them, don't ship\\\", \\\"build the stack, I'll land it\\\"). `playbooks/autopilot-stack.md`.\\n- **Session pickup.** Resuming or taking over a prior agent's in-flight work from a transcript, cloud-agent URL, or pushed branch. `playbooks/session-pickup.md`.\\n- **Pause safely.** Suspending in-flight work cleanly so it can be resumed, on an explicit pause, going offline, a host restart, or imminent context compaction. The complement to Session pickup. Full steps: `playbooks/pause-safely.md`.\\n- **Multi-phase or multi-PR plan.** Work that spans phases or stacked PRs. `playbooks/multi-phase-plan.md`.\\n- **Worktree and simulator cleanup.** Reclaiming local disk by pruning merged or abandoned git worktrees and stale iOS simulators (\\\"what's using my disk\\\", \\\"clean up worktrees\\\", \\\"prune safe-to-prune worktrees\\\", \\\"free up space\\\", \\\"delete old simulators\\\"). `playbooks/worktree-cleanup.md`.\\n- **Opening a PR.** Invoked at the end of every other playbook. `playbooks/opening-a-pr.md`.\\n\"\n    }\n  },\n  {\n    \"tool\": \"exec_command\",\n    \"args\": {\n      \"cmd\": \"cat skills/principle-type-system-discipline/SKILL.md skills/typescript-best-practices/references/patterns.md references/workflow-ownership.md references/model-roles.md references/orchestration-contract.md references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/bug-fix.md\",\n      \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3\",\n      \"max_output_tokens\": 16000\n    },\n    \"result\": {\n      \"chunk_id\": \"ba0924\",\n      \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000015041,\n      \"exit_code\": 0,\n      \"original_token_count\": 7952,\n      \"output\": \"---\\nname: principle-type-system-discipline\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when designing types, reviewing a function signature, or writing code in any statically-typed language. Make illegal states unrepresentable, brand semantic primitives, parse external data at boundaries, refuse to lie to the compiler, exhaust variants, derive from authoritative schemas.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Type System Discipline\\n\\nThe type checker is a proof assistant. Use it to eliminate impossible states, mismatched primitives, and unhandled variants at compile time. A case the types let you ignore becomes a runtime failure the compiler could have stopped. Prefer defining errors and special cases out of existence over proliferating handlers. Unrepresentable states, total functions, and interface redesign (the patterns below) are the tools.\\n\\nApplies to any typed language. Skills like `typescript-best-practices` ground it in specific syntax.\\n\\n**The patterns:**\\n\\n- **Make illegal states unrepresentable.** Model variants as sum types: discriminated unions in TypeScript, enums with payloads in Rust/Swift/Kotlin, sealed classes in Scala, ADTs in Haskell/OCaml. Don't model state as a bag of optional fields where contradictory combinations compile. A subtle anti-pattern: `{ completed: boolean; completedAt?: Date }` admits `completed: true; completedAt: undefined`, which is meaningless. Derive the boolean from a single source like `completedAt !== null`, or model the variants explicitly as `{ kind: 'open' } | { kind: 'done'; at: Date }`. If a bug forces the question \\\"wait, can this combination actually happen?\\\", the type is too loose.\\n- **Types are constructions, not restrictions.** Build the type up from the values you want instead of carving them out of a looser type with checks. The invariant that seems to need a refinement type is usually a construction away. A non-empty list is a head plus a rest, not a list with a length check. A valid time range is a start plus a duration, not two timestamps you must keep ordered. No representation is privileged. A list of pairs is an even-length list if you interpret it that way, so choose the shape that cannot build the illegal value and expose the interface callers need on top.\\n- **Brand semantic primitives.** `UserId` and `OrderId` are strings underneath but should not be interchangeable. Newtypes in Rust, opaque types in Swift, value classes in Kotlin, phantom types in Haskell, branded intersections in TypeScript. Validate once at creation, trust the type downstream.\\n- **External data is untyped until parsed.** RPC payloads, JSON, IPC messages, CLI args, config files, environment variables, database rows. Have a parse function at every boundary that turns unstructured input into the typed model. See the **boundary-discipline** principle skill for where to put validation.\\n- **Don't lie to the type system.** Casts, unsafe coercions, and assertion functions that bypass the compiler are latent runtime crashes. If the compiler can't prove a fact, prove it (validate, narrow, refine the model) or accept that the cast is a hazard.\\n- **Exhaustive matching is the compiler's job.** When you match on a sum type, the compiler must fail compilation if a new variant is added without handling. Use the idiom your language provides: `never`-typed binding in TypeScript, unannotated `match` in Rust, `-Wincomplete-patterns` in Haskell, sealed-class match exhaustiveness in Kotlin.\\n- **Derive types from authoritative schemas.** When a protocol buffer, OpenAPI spec, GraphQL schema, database migration, or design-system token file defines a shape, derive from it instead of hand-rolling a parallel type. See the **encode-lessons-in-structure** principle skill.\\n- **Strengthen a type only where partiality appears.** A runtime assertion, null check, or \\\"this should never happen\\\" throw marks the place a type is too weak. Push that check up into the type. Then stop. The type system's job is to track the cases each use site must handle, not to describe the data as precisely as possible. Prefer total functions. `sum` of an empty list is 0, so it takes the plain list. `head` of an empty list has no answer, so it demands the non-empty one.\\n\\n**The tests:**\\n\\n- \\\"Can I write a comment explaining when this combination of fields is valid?\\\" If yes, the type is too loose. Split it into a sum type.\\n- \\\"Do two of my function arguments share a primitive type but mean different things?\\\" Brand them.\\n- \\\"Where did this `any`, this `as`, this `assertNotNull` come from?\\\" Trace it to the boundary and validate there instead.\\n- \\\"If a new variant is added next month, will the compiler tell the next agent where to add a case?\\\" If no, the match isn't exhaustive.\\n- \\\"Is this type duplicating a shape another file owns?\\\" Derive instead.\\n- \\\"Am I strengthening this type to keep an operation total, or just to be more precise?\\\" If nothing would otherwise panic, keep the plain type.\\n\\n## Session capabilities and limits\\n\\nFollow [host capability discovery](../../references/host-capabilities.md) for source reads, authorized edits and compiler execution. The metadata above preserves the source invocation policy; do not assume the host enforces it.\\n\\nApply these patterns within the authorized task and exposed tools. If source access, writes or compiler execution are unavailable, provide a reduced proposal and state the missing checks. Identify the actual language limit when an invariant cannot be expressed in its types, and keep the required runtime validation explicit. Do not claim a compiler check ran without observed execution. Missing tools do not authorize installation or external actions.\\n# TypeScript patterns\\n\\nCode examples for each rule in `SKILL.md`. The underlying principles are language-agnostic. See the **type-system-discipline** and **boundary-discipline** principle skills.\\n\\n## Branded types\\n\\nBrand primitives so they can't be mixed up. Validate once at the boundary. Downstream code trusts the type.\\n\\n```ts\\ntype AgentId = string & { readonly __brand: \\\"AgentId\\\" };\\n\\nfunction parseAgentId(input: string): AgentId {\\n  if (!isUUID(input)) throw new Error(`Invalid agent id: ${input}`);\\n  return input as AgentId;\\n}\\n\\nfunction focusAgent(id: AgentId): void {\\n  /* input is trusted */\\n}\\n```\\n\\nMatch the `readonly __brand: 'X'` shape. Don't invent a new convention.\\n\\n## Discriminated unions\\n\\nModel variants with a literal discriminant. Every variant shares the field name and each variant's value is unique, so impossible combos can't be represented.\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't. Boolean + optionals lets contradictory states exist.\\ntype DiffState = { loading: boolean; diff?: GitDiff; error?: string };\\n\\n// Do. Only valid states exist.\\ntype DiffState =\\n  | { kind: \\\"loading\\\" }\\n  | { kind: \\\"ready\\\"; diff: GitDiff }\\n  | { kind: \\\"error\\\"; error: string };\\n```\\n\\nPick one discriminant name (`kind`, `type`, `tag`) and stick to it.\\n\\n## Constructive modeling\\n\\nBuild the type from parts that are all legal instead of restricting a loose type with runtime checks.\\n\\nNon-empty, via a variadic tuple:\\n\\n```ts\\ntype NonEmpty<T> = [T, ...T[]];\\n\\n// Don't: T[] plus a length check every caller must repeat\\nfunction pickWinner(entries: string[]): string {\\n  if (entries.length === 0) throw new Error(\\\"no entries\\\");\\n  return entries[Math.floor(Math.random() * entries.length)];\\n}\\n\\n// Do: an empty value of the type can't exist\\nfunction pickWinner(entries: NonEmpty<string>): string {\\n  return entries[Math.floor(Math.random() * entries.length)];\\n}\\n```\\n\\nWhere a plain `T[]` arrives, narrow once with a guard. The fact then travels in the type:\\n\\n```ts\\nconst isNonEmpty = <T>(arr: T[]): arr is NonEmpty<T> => arr.length > 0;\\n```\\n\\nEven length, as pairs:\\n\\n```ts\\ntype Pairs<T> = [T, T][];\\n```\\n\\nA time range, as start plus duration:\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't: a comment holds the invariant\\ntype TimeRange = { start: Date; end: Date }; // start <= end\\n\\n// Do: a negative range can't be written; derive end when needed\\ntype TimeRange = { start: Date; durationMs: number };\\n```\\n\\nKeep `durationMs` a plain number. Brand it (per Branded types) only if a raw number could be passed where a duration is expected, not by reflex. Pick the representation that makes the bad state unconstructable, then expose the reading you need on top (`pairs.flat()`, a `rangeEnd()` helper).\\n\\n## Simplest total type\\n\\nDon't strengthen everything. Keep `T[]` when every operation on it is total:\\n\\n```ts\\nconst sum = (xs: number[]) => xs.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0); // [] is 0, fine\\n```\\n\\nStrengthen when the loose type forces a lie at a use site. The tells are `!`, `arr[0] as T`, and a \\\"should never happen\\\" throw:\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't: partiality smuggled past the compiler\\nfunction newestSession(sessions: Session[]): Session {\\n  return sessions.at(0)!;\\n}\\n\\n// Do: strengthen the input; the assertion disappears\\nfunction newestSession(sessions: NonEmpty<Session>): Session {\\n  return sessions[0];\\n}\\n```\\n\\nWeakening the result to `Session | undefined` is the other total signature.\\n\\n## `unknown` over `any`\\n\\nExternal data is always `unknown`. Narrow before use.\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't\\nfunction handle(input: any) {\\n  return input.foo.bar;\\n}\\n\\n// Do\\nfunction handle(input: unknown) {\\n  if (typeof input === \\\"object\\\" && input !== null && \\\"foo\\\" in input) {\\n    // narrowed; compiler verifies access\\n  }\\n}\\n```\\n\\nExternal sources include RPC payloads, `JSON.parse`, `postMessage`, IPC, file contents, environment variables, database results.\\n\\n## Schemas before hand-rolled guards\\n\\nBefore writing a property-by-property type guard for external data, look for the repository's runtime schema library and existing schemas. Let one schema own validation and derive the TypeScript type from it. Do not maintain a schema, a duplicate interface, and a guard that can drift apart.\\n\\n```ts\\nimport { z } from \\\"zod\\\";\\n\\nconst UserSchema = z.object({\\n  id: z.string().uuid(),\\n  role: z.enum([\\\"admin\\\", \\\"member\\\"]),\\n});\\n\\ntype User = z.infer<typeof UserSchema>;\\n\\nfunction parseUser(input: unknown): User {\\n  return UserSchema.parse(input);\\n}\\n```\\n\\nUse `safeParse` when failure is an expected branch. Use the equivalent inference helper when the repository uses another schema library. Do not add a new schema dependency for one guard. This rule prefers the schema system the codebase already trusts.\\n\\n## No `as` casts\\n\\nEvery `as` is a potential runtime crash. Cast only after the type system has verified the claim.\\n\\n```ts\\nimport { z } from \\\"zod\\\";\\n\\n// Don't\\nconst user = data as User;\\n\\n// Don't\\nfunction isUser(data: unknown): data is User {\\n  return typeof data === \\\"object\\\" && data !== null && \\\"id\\\" in data;\\n}\\n\\n// Do\\nconst userSchema = z.object({ id: z.string(), name: z.string() });\\ntype User = z.infer<typeof userSchema>;\\n\\nfunction parseUser(data: unknown): User {\\n  return userSchema.parse(data);\\n}\\n```\\n\\nWhen the type comes first, annotate the validator with the type it proves. The compiler then rejects a validator that proves less than the type. Remove `name` from the object below and the assignment fails to compile.\\n\\n```ts\\ntype User = { id: string; name: string };\\n\\nconst userSchema: z.ZodType<User> = z.object({ id: z.string(), name: z.string() });\\n```\\n\\nWhen refactoring an `as` out of existing code, identify why TypeScript can't infer:\\n\\n- Missing discriminant: add one, switch to a discriminated union.\\n- Overly wide source type (e.g. `Record<string, unknown>`): narrow it.\\n- Untyped boundary: parse with the schema that owns the shape. Add a schema only where none exists.\\n- Genuinely inexpressible: use a branded type or `satisfies`.\\n\\n## Narrowing hierarchy\\n\\nFrom best to last-resort:\\n\\n1. **Discriminated union switch / if.** Compiler narrows automatically.\\n2. **`in` operator.** `\\\"key\\\" in obj` narrows to variants containing that key.\\n3. **`typeof` / `instanceof`.** For primitives and class instances.\\n4. **User-defined type guard.** When the above aren't enough.\\n5. **`as` cast.** Only after validation.\\n\\n```ts\\nfunction area(s: Shape): number {\\n  if (\\\"radius\\\" in s) return Math.PI * s.radius ** 2; // narrowed to circle\\n  return s.width * s.height; // narrowed to rect\\n}\\n```\\n\\n## Type guards\\n\\nA guard must actually verify the claim. A lying guard is worse than `as`.\\n\\n```ts\\nfunction isCircle(s: Shape): s is Shape & { kind: \\\"circle\\\" } {\\n  return s.kind === \\\"circle\\\";\\n}\\n```\\n\\nPrefer discriminant narrowing when possible.\\n\\n## Exhaustiveness\\n\\nIn default arms, assign the discriminant to a `never`-typed local.\\n\\n```ts\\n// Value-returning switch\\nfunction area(s: Shape): number {\\n  switch (s.kind) {\\n    case \\\"circle\\\":\\n      return Math.PI * s.radius ** 2;\\n    case \\\"rect\\\":\\n      return s.width * s.height;\\n    default: {\\n      const _exhaustive: never = s;\\n      return _exhaustive;\\n    }\\n  }\\n}\\n\\n// Void switch\\nfunction handle(s: Shape): void {\\n  switch (s.kind) {\\n    case \\\"circle\\\":\\n      drawCircle(s);\\n      break;\\n    case \\\"rect\\\":\\n      drawRect(s);\\n      break;\\n    default: {\\n      const _exhaustive: never = s;\\n      void _exhaustive;\\n    }\\n  }\\n}\\n```\\n\\nReturn-style in value-returning switches, void-style in statement switches.\\n\\n## `satisfies` over `as`\\n\\n`satisfies` validates without widening literal types.\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't. Widens, loses literal types.\\nconst config = { theme: \\\"dark\\\", cols: 3 } as Config;\\n\\n// Do. Validates AND preserves literal types.\\nconst config = { theme: \\\"dark\\\", cols: 3 } satisfies Config;\\n// config.theme is \\\"dark\\\" (literal), not string\\n```\\n\\n## Boundary validation\\n\\nValidate once where data crosses in. Trust types inside. See the **boundary-discipline** principle skill.\\n\\n- **Wire formats** (proto, JSON-RPC): parse with `ignoreUnknownFields` so forward-compatible changes don't break old clients.\\n- **Persisted JSON:** versioned blob with a try/catch around the parse.\\n- **Don't re-validate** deep in call chains.\\n\\n## Schema-derived types\\n\\nWhen a `.proto`, OpenAPI spec, GraphQL schema, or database migration already defines a shape, derive from the generated types instead of duplicating them.\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't. Duplicate shape, drifts when the schema changes.\\ntype CheckSummary = {\\n  totalCount: number;\\n  checks: { name: string; status: string }[];\\n};\\nfunction renderChecks(s: CheckSummary) {\\n  /* ... */\\n}\\n\\n// Do. Derive from the generated schema type.\\nimport type { ChecksMessage } from \\\"<generated module>\\\";\\nfunction renderChecks(s: Pick<ChecksMessage, \\\"totalCount\\\" | \\\"checks\\\">) {\\n  /* ... */\\n}\\n```\\n\\nReach for `Pick`, `Omit`, `Parameters`, `ReturnType`, `Awaited`, `typeof` before writing a new interface.\\n\\n## Object args\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't. Swap two args, still compiles.\\nopenFile(uri, {\\n  startLineNumber: 10,\\n  startColumn: 1,\\n  endLineNumber: 10,\\n  endColumn: 1,\\n});\\n\\n// Do. Order-independent, self-documenting.\\nopenFile({\\n  uri,\\n  selection: {\\n    startLineNumber: 10,\\n    startColumn: 1,\\n    endLineNumber: 10,\\n    endColumn: 1,\\n  },\\n});\\n```\\n\\nSkip on hot paths: per-frame render, tokenizers, parsers, anything in a tight loop where the allocation cost matters.\\n# Workflow ownership\\n\\nAn explicit pstack invocation gives pstack ownership of that workflow. An explicit Superpowers invocation gives Superpowers ownership. Do not run both controllers over the same assignment or let one silently replace the other's plan, review, or integration decisions.\\n\\nFor an unqualified request, D1 retains the already active workflow. With no active workflow, use host defaults and do not activate either orchestration mode automatically. If both pstack and Superpowers are explicitly requested, clarify ownership before launching workers or changing artifacts. Preserve the active controller across follow-ups unless the user changes ownership. A helper skill can advise the owner without becoming a second controller.\\n\\nThe owner assigns one coordinator. Workers produce isolated artifacts and evidence; the coordinator reviews and integrates them. Shared inventory and registry writes belong to the coordinator. Worker cancellation, stale results or unavailable tools do not transfer that ownership automatically.\\n\\nHost permissions and user authorization constrain every owner. Local structural tests, documented APIs and declared cases provide no host pass. Explicit reduced or blocked results remain visible beside the full expectation.\\n# Model selection for portable roles\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](host-capabilities.md) before assigning a role. These [poteto](agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and [comment reviewer](agent-prompts/comment-sicko.md) prompts are instructions supplied to an exposed worker operation. Reading them does not register a custom agent or authorize delegation.\\n\\n| Role | Default requested model | Observed model | Supported effort | Fallback disclosure |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| poteto-agent | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\n| comment-sicko | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\n\\nFor each assignment, record requested model and effort, observed model and effort, supported effort values and their source, worker identity, and fallback disclosure. Use `unknown` for information the operation does not expose. `inherit-parent` records a selection policy, not an observed model name. Do not silently convert a requested model into an actual model.\\n\\nSelect an explicit model only from current exposed tool documentation or observed enumeration results. If enumeration is missing, disclose that limit and retain `inherit-parent`. Documentation examples are not a list of available models. Use only an effort supported for the selected model by the current operation; do not carry an incompatible inherited effort into an explicit selection.\\n\\nIf a selected model or effort is rejected, retain the rejection. Use an authorized inherited or supported alternative only after disclosing the changed selection. If the task requires that exact selection, stop and record blocked. A fallback never proves the original selection succeeded.\\n\\nIdentical observed models mean no model diversity. Different roles can supply distinct instructions on the same model. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified, even when requested model names differ. Different observed models support a model-diversity statement only for those observed assignments. Worker independence still requires successful spawn and result inspection.\\n\\nWhen workers are unavailable, apply an authorized role sequentially and label reduced capability. Preserve scope and permission boundaries. Do not claim independent review, background execution, or full worker support from sequential role switching.\\n\\nThe cached documentation from https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents describes inheritance and optional local agent configuration. It is context only. This package writes no local custom-agent configuration and claims no current host model or worker support from that documentation.\\n# Assignment and integration contract\\n\\nBefore dispatch, the coordinator creates an immutable assignment with `id`, `scope`, `inputRevision`, `outputOwner`, `isolatedOutputPath`, `role`, `retryCount`, `terminalStatus`, and `evidenceLinks`. Use the full Git SHA for inputRevision, an exact owned path, and an explicit role. Start retryCount at 0 and terminalStatus at `pending`. Preserve every attempt as an append-only event under the same assignment ID; update status through events, never rewrite the original scope, revision, owner or path. A changed scope or revision requires a new assignment ID that links its predecessor.\\n\\nThe coordinator checks authorization and observed host bindings before dispatch. One path has one writer. Reject overlapping paths before work starts; assign distinct paths instead. Workers write only their isolated output and report results. Only the coordinator integrates shared files, inventory, registry, or other workers' output.\\n\\nAccept a result only when its assignment identity and input SHA match, its artifacts exist, and required method evidence was observed. Reject stale revisions. Permit at most one retry for missing required SHA or method evidence, after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming the same permissions still apply. A retry count of 1 exhausts this allowance. Denied permissions, unknown action outcomes, and missing artifacts never become success by retry policy.\\n\\nTerminal states are `completed`, `blocked`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. A completion event links the exact result and evidence. Stop a cancelled worker through the exposed stop binding when available, then inspect its terminal state. Quarantine any partial artifacts; cancellation does not authorize their integration. If stop cannot be observed, record that limit and do not claim termination. Report interrupted execution explicitly.\\n\\nBefore an external action, record explicit permission, a stable action identity, the intended destination and the last observed outcome. After interruption, inspect whether that identity already completed. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to the destination. If the outcome is unknown, block replay. Do not infer permission from upstream autonomy instructions.\\n\\nHistory and persistence have separate bindings. Keep assignment events and result links in an authorized durable location and verify readback. If unavailable, declare the gap and offer an authorized local handoff as reduced capability; never claim unattended recovery or cross-session continuity. All host statuses remain `not-run` until actual observations exist.\\n\\n## Structured Arena and Swarm receipts\\n\\nA method receipt is JSON with schemaVersion 1, kind `arena` or `swarm`, inputRevision, assignments, reviewArtifact, reviewedBy and declaredOrder. The inputRevision is the actual task repository revision, distinct from the packageCommit identifying these instructions. It must match the evidence record. The declared order precedes every dispatch. reviewArtifact records the qualitative outcome review and reviewedBy identifies its reviewer. All referenced artifacts must exist and be listed in the evidence record.\\n\\nEach assignment records id, workerId, inputRevision, outputOwner, outputPath, role, status, startedOrder, completedOrder, requestedModel, actualModel, retryCount, method, taskArtifact, rubricProvided, resultRevision, resultMethod and outputSha256. Result revision and method must match the assignment, and the inspected output bytes must match their digest. Worker and assignment identities are distinct. outputOwner equals workerId; output directories do not overlap. Integer order values record the observed dispatch and completion sequence, not estimated time. The method has positive sampleCount, sampleDefinition and order. Missing model observations use unknown. The validator requires assignment-bound spawn and result inspection, and isolated-write for output-producing candidates. A read-only judge may return its result through the coordinator's authorized artifact capture. A cancelled result needs bound stop, terminal inspection and quarantine observations. No operation string alone establishes a method.\\n\\nArena adds candidates, judge, rubricArtifact and synthesis. At least two candidates share the same taskArtifact, finish before a distinct fresh judge starts and do not receive the parent rubric. Synthesis has order, inspectedCandidates, artifact and verificationArtifact; it follows completed judgment and accounts for every candidate. The review artifact assesses scoring, base selection, grafts, rejections and verification quality. File presence and phase ordering do not establish the quality of those decisions.\\n\\nSwarm adds partition, race, cancelled and quarantined. Partition declares requiredSlices, results mapping each slice to its assignment, and gaps. A full coverage claim requires all slices with completed inspected outputs; gaps remain explicit reduced or blocked work. Race declares rule, assignments, winner and ruleArtifact. first-pass must select the first completed survivor; rank-all needs all results; best-of retains an explicit reviewed selection. Cancelled assignments must be quarantined and cannot enter the aggregate. The combined partition-and-race case requires both methods and its cancellation exercise. The partition walkthrough exercises only its declared slices; it does not acquire an unrelated race requirement.\\n\\nThe three-file Swarm walkthrough requires exactly three declared slices and three completed results. Its partition-only receipt uses race null and empty cancellation, quarantine and failureExercises arrays. The combined case also records failureExercises. Each event has assignmentId, kind, attempt, order, reason and evidence, with bound result-rejection or terminal inspection. Rejected missing-revision or missing-method evidence uses attempts 0 then 1; a second invalid result exhausts the retry and leaves a failed or blocked assignment. A separate failed or blocked dropout is disclosed. These failed outputs cannot satisfy required coverage. The final assignment result metadata records the inspected terminal summary; rejection-event artifacts retain the invalid attempts.\\n# Poteto agent prompt\\n\\nUse this role for `/poteto-mode` and requests for poteto's style. Supply these instructions to a fresh independent worker for each new task when the session exposes and permits that operation. Resume a worker only in the strict cases named by poteto-mode's Subagents section. Generic worker routing is acceptable only when it carries this entire prompt and the required mode read; otherwise it skips the style contract.\\n\\nBefore any work, read [poteto-mode's SKILL.md](../../skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md) in full, including its inline Principles index. Navigate to the leaf `principle-*` skill whenever you apply that principle. If the mode or an applicable leaf is unavailable, report that missing dependency and stop dependent work. Do not reconstruct the mode from a summary.\\n\\nFollow [model roles](../model-roles.md) and [session capabilities](../host-capabilities.md). This prompt does not register an agent or promise background execution. Record requested and observed models separately. Preserve user authorization and host permissions throughout the mode. An authorized sequential fallback uses the same full read and discloses reduced capability; it does not count as an independent worker.\\n### Bug fix\\n\\n**You own this task. Plan, review, verify.** Delegate investigation and the fix to subagents, stay in the lead. Read the complete [mode](../SKILL.md), applied principle leaves and routed skills. Record these six steps before task-specific todos.\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md) and the [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Supply the complete [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) to ad-hoc investigators and code delegates; routed skills retain their own roles. Assign exact input revisions, scopes, ownership and success criteria. Inspect artifacts and review the diff yourself. Missing independent workers allow authorized sequential work only as reduced capability. Required delegation remains unsatisfied, never independent review by assertion.\\n\\nBe scientific. Every shipped line traces to runtime evidence. Belt-and-suspenders that \\\"might help\\\" is a hypothesis, not a fix. It does not ship. When evidence refutes a hypothesis, revert what it motivated. The smallest change the evidence justifies ships, nothing more.\\n\\n1. Reproduce it yourself on the matching surface via the control skill in the mode's Non-negotiables, even when a debug or instrumentation protocol says to ask the user to reproduce. Ask the user only with a stated, specific reason the control surface cannot reach the target, and only after driving it as far as it goes. If it won't reproduce directly, synthesize the trigger, tighten conditions, or instrument until it fires. Record the exact symptom, trigger, surface, revision, command or action, exit status where available, and output before changing production code. Missing control companions or denied execution leave the original reproduction unverified. A proxy check cannot satisfy the matching-surface gate.\\n2. Binary-search the cause. Form candidate hypotheses, then rule them out until one survives. Seed them with [how](../../how/SKILL.md) over the affected subsystem and [why](../../why/SKILL.md) for regression history, using their complete prompts and source contracts. Each pass takes the split that cuts the most remaining problem space, gets runtime evidence and eliminates candidates. When program state is unclear, add authorized instrumentation or logging and read it as the code runs. Don't guess. Drive a long or stubborn hunt with an exposed, authorized repeated-execution operation. If unavailable, perform bounded in-session iterations and disclose reduced capability, without claiming a background loop. Confirm the surviving mechanism with runtime evidence before the step-3 architect/interrogate fan-out.\\n3. Plan the fix. If it crosses a function boundary, [architect](../../architect/SKILL.md) first, using its complete exploration, runner, rationale and screening contracts. Contested design follows the mode's [interrogate](../../interrogate/SKILL.md) gate. Delegate implementation to a subagent using the configured bug-fix role with a specific scope. The portable default is inherited model and effort. The canonical default `grok-4.7-xhigh-fast` is historical provenance, not current availability. Select a configured model only when supported by exposed tools and observed; retain rejected selections and disclosed fallbacks. Unknown actual models prove no diversity. No required independent operation means that gate remains blocked.\\n4. Verify on the same surface. The original repro now passes. Retain the same trigger and conditions and record the actual output after the bounded root-cause fix. \\\"Inconclusive\\\" or wrong-surface is not a pass. Flag it. Unit tests show branch behavior, not bug absence. Keep denied controls, missing companions and unrun checks explicit.\\n5. Stage the commits so the failing repro lands before the fix in git history. See [tdd](../../tdd/SKILL.md) for the failing-test-first cadence when the bug has a cheap local test path. Skip adding that test when it would be expensive, integration-heavy or unclear, and state the reason and closest executable regression check. This is the canonical [sequence-verifiable-units](../../principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md) principle skill, the failing test first and the fix on top. Commits, rebases and history changes require actual authorization and available operations. Without them, preserve the failing-before/passing-after artifacts and ordered delivery plan; do not claim that sequence landed.\\n6. Run [Opening a PR](opening-a-pr.md) in full when its dependency and required capabilities are available and authorized. Missing playbook or companion gates stop dependent delivery. Return checked local artifacts and the exact unperformed actions otherwise. PR creation and other external writes require explicit authorization. Opening a PR does not implicitly start Babysit.\\n\\n**Reply:** what was broken, root cause, fix, how you verified. Paste failing-then-passing repro output verbatim. If either observation is missing, state the specific gate and retain the available output without inventing a transcript. Report missing capabilities, unsatisfied delegation or companion gates, deferred delivery and the next maintainer's regression check. Apply the local [unslop](../../unslop/SKILL.md) contract.\\n\"\n    }\n  },\n  {\n    \"tool\": \"exec_command\",\n    \"args\": {\n      \"cmd\": \"cat skills/principle-type-system-discipline/SKILL.md skills/typescript-best-practices/references/patterns.md skills/unslop/SKILL.md\",\n      \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3\",\n      \"max_output_tokens\": 10000\n    },\n    \"result\": {\n      \"chunk_id\": \"1b33ba\",\n      \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000006208,\n      \"exit_code\": 0,\n      \"original_token_count\": 5218,\n      \"output\": \"---\\nname: principle-type-system-discipline\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when designing types, reviewing a function signature, or writing code in any statically-typed language. Make illegal states unrepresentable, brand semantic primitives, parse external data at boundaries, refuse to lie to the compiler, exhaust variants, derive from authoritative schemas.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Type System Discipline\\n\\nThe type checker is a proof assistant. Use it to eliminate impossible states, mismatched primitives, and unhandled variants at compile time. A case the types let you ignore becomes a runtime failure the compiler could have stopped. Prefer defining errors and special cases out of existence over proliferating handlers. Unrepresentable states, total functions, and interface redesign (the patterns below) are the tools.\\n\\nApplies to any typed language. Skills like `typescript-best-practices` ground it in specific syntax.\\n\\n**The patterns:**\\n\\n- **Make illegal states unrepresentable.** Model variants as sum types: discriminated unions in TypeScript, enums with payloads in Rust/Swift/Kotlin, sealed classes in Scala, ADTs in Haskell/OCaml. Don't model state as a bag of optional fields where contradictory combinations compile. A subtle anti-pattern: `{ completed: boolean; completedAt?: Date }` admits `completed: true; completedAt: undefined`, which is meaningless. Derive the boolean from a single source like `completedAt !== null`, or model the variants explicitly as `{ kind: 'open' } | { kind: 'done'; at: Date }`. If a bug forces the question \\\"wait, can this combination actually happen?\\\", the type is too loose.\\n- **Types are constructions, not restrictions.** Build the type up from the values you want instead of carving them out of a looser type with checks. The invariant that seems to need a refinement type is usually a construction away. A non-empty list is a head plus a rest, not a list with a length check. A valid time range is a start plus a duration, not two timestamps you must keep ordered. No representation is privileged. A list of pairs is an even-length list if you interpret it that way, so choose the shape that cannot build the illegal value and expose the interface callers need on top.\\n- **Brand semantic primitives.** `UserId` and `OrderId` are strings underneath but should not be interchangeable. Newtypes in Rust, opaque types in Swift, value classes in Kotlin, phantom types in Haskell, branded intersections in TypeScript. Validate once at creation, trust the type downstream.\\n- **External data is untyped until parsed.** RPC payloads, JSON, IPC messages, CLI args, config files, environment variables, database rows. Have a parse function at every boundary that turns unstructured input into the typed model. See the **boundary-discipline** principle skill for where to put validation.\\n- **Don't lie to the type system.** Casts, unsafe coercions, and assertion functions that bypass the compiler are latent runtime crashes. If the compiler can't prove a fact, prove it (validate, narrow, refine the model) or accept that the cast is a hazard.\\n- **Exhaustive matching is the compiler's job.** When you match on a sum type, the compiler must fail compilation if a new variant is added without handling. Use the idiom your language provides: `never`-typed binding in TypeScript, unannotated `match` in Rust, `-Wincomplete-patterns` in Haskell, sealed-class match exhaustiveness in Kotlin.\\n- **Derive types from authoritative schemas.** When a protocol buffer, OpenAPI spec, GraphQL schema, database migration, or design-system token file defines a shape, derive from it instead of hand-rolling a parallel type. See the **encode-lessons-in-structure** principle skill.\\n- **Strengthen a type only where partiality appears.** A runtime assertion, null check, or \\\"this should never happen\\\" throw marks the place a type is too weak. Push that check up into the type. Then stop. The type system's job is to track the cases each use site must handle, not to describe the data as precisely as possible. Prefer total functions. `sum` of an empty list is 0, so it takes the plain list. `head` of an empty list has no answer, so it demands the non-empty one.\\n\\n**The tests:**\\n\\n- \\\"Can I write a comment explaining when this combination of fields is valid?\\\" If yes, the type is too loose. Split it into a sum type.\\n- \\\"Do two of my function arguments share a primitive type but mean different things?\\\" Brand them.\\n- \\\"Where did this `any`, this `as`, this `assertNotNull` come from?\\\" Trace it to the boundary and validate there instead.\\n- \\\"If a new variant is added next month, will the compiler tell the next agent where to add a case?\\\" If no, the match isn't exhaustive.\\n- \\\"Is this type duplicating a shape another file owns?\\\" Derive instead.\\n- \\\"Am I strengthening this type to keep an operation total, or just to be more precise?\\\" If nothing would otherwise panic, keep the plain type.\\n\\n## Session capabilities and limits\\n\\nFollow [host capability discovery](../../references/host-capabilities.md) for source reads, authorized edits and compiler execution. The metadata above preserves the source invocation policy; do not assume the host enforces it.\\n\\nApply these patterns within the authorized task and exposed tools. If source access, writes or compiler execution are unavailable, provide a reduced proposal and state the missing checks. Identify the actual language limit when an invariant cannot be expressed in its types, and keep the required runtime validation explicit. Do not claim a compiler check ran without observed execution. Missing tools do not authorize installation or external actions.\\n# TypeScript patterns\\n\\nCode examples for each rule in `SKILL.md`. The underlying principles are language-agnostic. See the **type-system-discipline** and **boundary-discipline** principle skills.\\n\\n## Branded types\\n\\nBrand primitives so they can't be mixed up. Validate once at the boundary. Downstream code trusts the type.\\n\\n```ts\\ntype AgentId = string & { readonly __brand: \\\"AgentId\\\" };\\n\\nfunction parseAgentId(input: string): AgentId {\\n  if (!isUUID(input)) throw new Error(`Invalid agent id: ${input}`);\\n  return input as AgentId;\\n}\\n\\nfunction focusAgent(id: AgentId): void {\\n  /* input is trusted */\\n}\\n```\\n\\nMatch the `readonly __brand: 'X'` shape. Don't invent a new convention.\\n\\n## Discriminated unions\\n\\nModel variants with a literal discriminant. Every variant shares the field name and each variant's value is unique, so impossible combos can't be represented.\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't. Boolean + optionals lets contradictory states exist.\\ntype DiffState = { loading: boolean; diff?: GitDiff; error?: string };\\n\\n// Do. Only valid states exist.\\ntype DiffState =\\n  | { kind: \\\"loading\\\" }\\n  | { kind: \\\"ready\\\"; diff: GitDiff }\\n  | { kind: \\\"error\\\"; error: string };\\n```\\n\\nPick one discriminant name (`kind`, `type`, `tag`) and stick to it.\\n\\n## Constructive modeling\\n\\nBuild the type from parts that are all legal instead of restricting a loose type with runtime checks.\\n\\nNon-empty, via a variadic tuple:\\n\\n```ts\\ntype NonEmpty<T> = [T, ...T[]];\\n\\n// Don't: T[] plus a length check every caller must repeat\\nfunction pickWinner(entries: string[]): string {\\n  if (entries.length === 0) throw new Error(\\\"no entries\\\");\\n  return entries[Math.floor(Math.random() * entries.length)];\\n}\\n\\n// Do: an empty value of the type can't exist\\nfunction pickWinner(entries: NonEmpty<string>): string {\\n  return entries[Math.floor(Math.random() * entries.length)];\\n}\\n```\\n\\nWhere a plain `T[]` arrives, narrow once with a guard. The fact then travels in the type:\\n\\n```ts\\nconst isNonEmpty = <T>(arr: T[]): arr is NonEmpty<T> => arr.length > 0;\\n```\\n\\nEven length, as pairs:\\n\\n```ts\\ntype Pairs<T> = [T, T][];\\n```\\n\\nA time range, as start plus duration:\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't: a comment holds the invariant\\ntype TimeRange = { start: Date; end: Date }; // start <= end\\n\\n// Do: a negative range can't be written; derive end when needed\\ntype TimeRange = { start: Date; durationMs: number };\\n```\\n\\nKeep `durationMs` a plain number. Brand it (per Branded types) only if a raw number could be passed where a duration is expected, not by reflex. Pick the representation that makes the bad state unconstructable, then expose the reading you need on top (`pairs.flat()`, a `rangeEnd()` helper).\\n\\n## Simplest total type\\n\\nDon't strengthen everything. Keep `T[]` when every operation on it is total:\\n\\n```ts\\nconst sum = (xs: number[]) => xs.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0); // [] is 0, fine\\n```\\n\\nStrengthen when the loose type forces a lie at a use site. The tells are `!`, `arr[0] as T`, and a \\\"should never happen\\\" throw:\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't: partiality smuggled past the compiler\\nfunction newestSession(sessions: Session[]): Session {\\n  return sessions.at(0)!;\\n}\\n\\n// Do: strengthen the input; the assertion disappears\\nfunction newestSession(sessions: NonEmpty<Session>): Session {\\n  return sessions[0];\\n}\\n```\\n\\nWeakening the result to `Session | undefined` is the other total signature.\\n\\n## `unknown` over `any`\\n\\nExternal data is always `unknown`. Narrow before use.\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't\\nfunction handle(input: any) {\\n  return input.foo.bar;\\n}\\n\\n// Do\\nfunction handle(input: unknown) {\\n  if (typeof input === \\\"object\\\" && input !== null && \\\"foo\\\" in input) {\\n    // narrowed; compiler verifies access\\n  }\\n}\\n```\\n\\nExternal sources include RPC payloads, `JSON.parse`, `postMessage`, IPC, file contents, environment variables, database results.\\n\\n## Schemas before hand-rolled guards\\n\\nBefore writing a property-by-property type guard for external data, look for the repository's runtime schema library and existing schemas. Let one schema own validation and derive the TypeScript type from it. Do not maintain a schema, a duplicate interface, and a guard that can drift apart.\\n\\n```ts\\nimport { z } from \\\"zod\\\";\\n\\nconst UserSchema = z.object({\\n  id: z.string().uuid(),\\n  role: z.enum([\\\"admin\\\", \\\"member\\\"]),\\n});\\n\\ntype User = z.infer<typeof UserSchema>;\\n\\nfunction parseUser(input: unknown): User {\\n  return UserSchema.parse(input);\\n}\\n```\\n\\nUse `safeParse` when failure is an expected branch. Use the equivalent inference helper when the repository uses another schema library. Do not add a new schema dependency for one guard. This rule prefers the schema system the codebase already trusts.\\n\\n## No `as` casts\\n\\nEvery `as` is a potential runtime crash. Cast only after the type system has verified the claim.\\n\\n```ts\\nimport { z } from \\\"zod\\\";\\n\\n// Don't\\nconst user = data as User;\\n\\n// Don't\\nfunction isUser(data: unknown): data is User {\\n  return typeof data === \\\"object\\\" && data !== null && \\\"id\\\" in data;\\n}\\n\\n// Do\\nconst userSchema = z.object({ id: z.string(), name: z.string() });\\ntype User = z.infer<typeof userSchema>;\\n\\nfunction parseUser(data: unknown): User {\\n  return userSchema.parse(data);\\n}\\n```\\n\\nWhen the type comes first, annotate the validator with the type it proves. The compiler then rejects a validator that proves less than the type. Remove `name` from the object below and the assignment fails to compile.\\n\\n```ts\\ntype User = { id: string; name: string };\\n\\nconst userSchema: z.ZodType<User> = z.object({ id: z.string(), name: z.string() });\\n```\\n\\nWhen refactoring an `as` out of existing code, identify why TypeScript can't infer:\\n\\n- Missing discriminant: add one, switch to a discriminated union.\\n- Overly wide source type (e.g. `Record<string, unknown>`): narrow it.\\n- Untyped boundary: parse with the schema that owns the shape. Add a schema only where none exists.\\n- Genuinely inexpressible: use a branded type or `satisfies`.\\n\\n## Narrowing hierarchy\\n\\nFrom best to last-resort:\\n\\n1. **Discriminated union switch / if.** Compiler narrows automatically.\\n2. **`in` operator.** `\\\"key\\\" in obj` narrows to variants containing that key.\\n3. **`typeof` / `instanceof`.** For primitives and class instances.\\n4. **User-defined type guard.** When the above aren't enough.\\n5. **`as` cast.** Only after validation.\\n\\n```ts\\nfunction area(s: Shape): number {\\n  if (\\\"radius\\\" in s) return Math.PI * s.radius ** 2; // narrowed to circle\\n  return s.width * s.height; // narrowed to rect\\n}\\n```\\n\\n## Type guards\\n\\nA guard must actually verify the claim. A lying guard is worse than `as`.\\n\\n```ts\\nfunction isCircle(s: Shape): s is Shape & { kind: \\\"circle\\\" } {\\n  return s.kind === \\\"circle\\\";\\n}\\n```\\n\\nPrefer discriminant narrowing when possible.\\n\\n## Exhaustiveness\\n\\nIn default arms, assign the discriminant to a `never`-typed local.\\n\\n```ts\\n// Value-returning switch\\nfunction area(s: Shape): number {\\n  switch (s.kind) {\\n    case \\\"circle\\\":\\n      return Math.PI * s.radius ** 2;\\n    case \\\"rect\\\":\\n      return s.width * s.height;\\n    default: {\\n      const _exhaustive: never = s;\\n      return _exhaustive;\\n    }\\n  }\\n}\\n\\n// Void switch\\nfunction handle(s: Shape): void {\\n  switch (s.kind) {\\n    case \\\"circle\\\":\\n      drawCircle(s);\\n      break;\\n    case \\\"rect\\\":\\n      drawRect(s);\\n      break;\\n    default: {\\n      const _exhaustive: never = s;\\n      void _exhaustive;\\n    }\\n  }\\n}\\n```\\n\\nReturn-style in value-returning switches, void-style in statement switches.\\n\\n## `satisfies` over `as`\\n\\n`satisfies` validates without widening literal types.\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't. Widens, loses literal types.\\nconst config = { theme: \\\"dark\\\", cols: 3 } as Config;\\n\\n// Do. Validates AND preserves literal types.\\nconst config = { theme: \\\"dark\\\", cols: 3 } satisfies Config;\\n// config.theme is \\\"dark\\\" (literal), not string\\n```\\n\\n## Boundary validation\\n\\nValidate once where data crosses in. Trust types inside. See the **boundary-discipline** principle skill.\\n\\n- **Wire formats** (proto, JSON-RPC): parse with `ignoreUnknownFields` so forward-compatible changes don't break old clients.\\n- **Persisted JSON:** versioned blob with a try/catch around the parse.\\n- **Don't re-validate** deep in call chains.\\n\\n## Schema-derived types\\n\\nWhen a `.proto`, OpenAPI spec, GraphQL schema, or database migration already defines a shape, derive from the generated types instead of duplicating them.\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't. Duplicate shape, drifts when the schema changes.\\ntype CheckSummary = {\\n  totalCount: number;\\n  checks: { name: string; status: string }[];\\n};\\nfunction renderChecks(s: CheckSummary) {\\n  /* ... */\\n}\\n\\n// Do. Derive from the generated schema type.\\nimport type { ChecksMessage } from \\\"<generated module>\\\";\\nfunction renderChecks(s: Pick<ChecksMessage, \\\"totalCount\\\" | \\\"checks\\\">) {\\n  /* ... */\\n}\\n```\\n\\nReach for `Pick`, `Omit`, `Parameters`, `ReturnType`, `Awaited`, `typeof` before writing a new interface.\\n\\n## Object args\\n\\n```ts\\n// Don't. Swap two args, still compiles.\\nopenFile(uri, {\\n  startLineNumber: 10,\\n  startColumn: 1,\\n  endLineNumber: 10,\\n  endColumn: 1,\\n});\\n\\n// Do. Order-independent, self-documenting.\\nopenFile({\\n  uri,\\n  selection: {\\n    startLineNumber: 10,\\n    startColumn: 1,\\n    endLineNumber: 10,\\n    endColumn: 1,\\n  },\\n});\\n```\\n\\nSkip on hot paths: per-frame render, tokenizers, parsers, anything in a tight loop where the allocation cost matters.\\n---\\nname: unslop\\ndescription: Cut AI tells from any writing. Must always apply.\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Unslop\\n\\nEdit text to remove AI patterns.\\n\\n## Process\\n\\n1. Scan for the patterns below.\\n2. Rewrite. Preserve meaning, match intended tone.\\n\\n## Patterns to detect and fix\\n\\nRule numbers are stable ids that other skills cite. A removed rule leaves a gap.\\n\\n### Content\\n\\n3. **Superficial -ing phrases.** \\\"highlighting...\\\", \\\"ensuring...\\\", \\\"reflecting...\\\", \\\"showcasing...\\\", \\\"fostering...\\\". Delete or expand with real sources.\\n5. **Vague attributions.** \\\"Experts believe\\\", \\\"Industry reports suggest\\\", \\\"Some critics argue\\\". Name the source or delete.\\n\\n### Language\\n\\n7. **AI vocabulary.** Additionally, crucial, delve, enduring, enhance, fostering, garner, interplay, intricate, landscape (abstract), pivotal, showcase, tapestry (abstract), testament, underscore, vibrant. Replace with plain words.\\n8. **Fancy ways to say \\\"is\\\".** \\\"serves as\\\", \\\"stands as\\\", \\\"boasts\\\", \\\"features\\\". Just say \\\"is\\\" or \\\"has\\\".\\n9. **\\\"Not just X, but Y.\\\"** State the point directly instead.\\n10. **Rule of three.** Forcing ideas into groups of three. Use the natural number.\\n11. **Synonym cycling.** Protagonist, main character, central figure, hero all in one paragraph. Pick one, repeat it.\\n12. **False ranges.** \\\"from X to Y\\\" where X and Y aren't on a meaningful scale. List topics directly.\\n\\n### Style\\n\\n13. **Em dash overuse.** Avoid em dashes entirely. Use periods or commas only (no parentheses, no en dashes, no hyphen-as-dash substitutes). If a thought needs separation, end the sentence or use a comma.\\n14. **Colon overuse.** Colons are fine before a list or example. Not as mid-sentence connectors. \\\"If you're coming from traditional automation: instead of registering event handlers, you describe conditions\\\" adds nothing with the colon. Rewrite to let the point stand on its own without comparison framing. \\\"Describing when the scheduler should fire works best as plain English.\\\" Same meaning, no crutch punctuation.\\n15. **Boldface overuse.** Don't bold every proper noun or acronym.\\n16. **Inline-header lists.** The tell is a bold label and colon that restates the line: \\\"**Performance:** Performance improved...\\\". Convert those to prose. A bold lead-in that ends in a period, names the item, and is followed by genuinely new detail (\\\"**Schema in TypeScript.** Tables live in one file.\\\") is fine, not a tell.\\n17. **Title case headings.** Use sentence case.\\n18. **Decorative emojis.** Remove from headings and bullets.\\n19. **Curly quotes.** Replace with straight quotes.\\n\\n### Communication artifacts\\n\\n20. **Chatbot phrases.** \\\"I hope this helps!\\\", \\\"Let me know if...\\\", \\\"Of course!\\\", \\\"Certainly!\\\", \\\"Found the smoking gun!\\\" Remove.\\n22. **Sycophantic tone.** \\\"Great question! You're absolutely right!\\\" Respond directly.\\n\\n### Filler\\n\\n23. **Filler phrases.** \\\"In order to\\\" becomes \\\"To\\\". \\\"Due to the fact that\\\" becomes \\\"Because\\\". \\\"It is important to note that\\\" gets deleted.\\n24. **Excessive hedging.** \\\"could potentially possibly be argued that it might\\\" becomes \\\"may\\\".\\n25. **Generic conclusions.** \\\"The future looks bright.\\\" State specific plans or facts.\\n\\n### Jargon\\n\\n26. **Abstract metaphor nouns.** Substrate, wedge, vector, locus, vantage, nexus, primitive (as noun), harness (as metaphor), surface (as in \\\"API surface\\\"), bedrock, scaffolding (as metaphor), modality, paradigm, gold-plating, ratchet (as metaphor), evacuate (for moving code), endgame, north star, flywheel. These read as technical but usually have a plainer concrete word. \\\"Substrate\\\" becomes \\\"base\\\". \\\"Wedge in\\\" becomes \\\"add\\\". \\\"Vector\\\" becomes \\\"way\\\" or \\\"method\\\". \\\"Gold-plating\\\" becomes \\\"more than the job needs\\\". \\\"Ratchet\\\" becomes the mechanism's real name or \\\"a limit that only tightens\\\". \\\"Evacuate\\\" becomes \\\"move out\\\". \\\"Endgame\\\" becomes \\\"the last phase\\\". Pick the concrete word.\\n\\n### Plain speech\\n\\n27. **Say what it does, not how it feels.** \\\"the database stays close at hand\\\", \\\"SQL you can read\\\", \\\"types that follow your schema\\\" name a feeling. The fix names the mechanism or a number: \\\"`.toSQL()` returns the exact string sent to the database\\\", \\\"a column rename fails the build\\\". Ask what the sentence tells the reader to do or know, then write that. If you can't restate it as a concrete instruction, fact, or number, cut it. One more check: if the sentence could appear unchanged in another project's docs, it says nothing about this one. Cut it.\\n28. **Shorten or split dense sentences.** If the reader has to backtrack to parse a sentence, break it in two or drop clauses. One idea per sentence.\\n29. **Active voice.** Prefer it. Catch \\\"is/are/was/were + past participle\\\" and name the actor: \\\"queries are validated\\\" becomes \\\"the compiler validates queries\\\", \\\"the file is parsed by the loader\\\" becomes \\\"the loader parses the file\\\". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or genuinely doesn't matter.\\n30. **Cut adverbs, or use a stronger verb.** \\\"runs quickly\\\" becomes \\\"is fast\\\" or the number. \\\"significantly improves\\\" becomes the measured delta. An adverb propping up a weak verb means the verb is wrong.\\n31. **Prefer the plain word.** \\\"utilize\\\" becomes \\\"use\\\", \\\"leverage\\\" becomes \\\"use\\\", \\\"facilitate\\\" becomes \\\"help\\\", \\\"numerous\\\" becomes \\\"many\\\", \\\"in the event that\\\" becomes \\\"if\\\". The fancier synonym is rarely clearer.\\n32. **Mannered prose.** Metaphor or flourish where a literal phrase exists: aphorisms (\\\"wire it or delete it\\\"), rhetorical fragments for effect, personified code (\\\"the plan holds it\\\"), figurative verbs (\\\"rides along\\\", \\\"stands on\\\"), stock framing phrases. \\\"A dial worth turning\\\" becomes \\\"a parameter worth varying\\\". Say what you mean. Rule 26 covers the metaphor nouns.\\n33. **Over-compression.** Dropped articles, verbless fragments, symbol-speak, and abbreviations that make the reader decode instead of read. \\\"Parser rejects bad date → exit 2, no write\\\" becomes \\\"The parser rejects a bad date, exits with code 2, and writes nothing.\\\" Write whole sentences with their articles and verbs, and spell out arrows and abbreviations.\\n\"\n    }\n  },\n  {\n    \"tool\": \"exec_command\",\n    \"args\": {\n      \"cmd\": \"find .. -name AGENTS.md -print; Treat agreement as supporting evidence only after checking independence, the observed models and the underlying artifacts.\\n\\n**Fresh subagents by default.** Give new work to a fresh subagent with consolidated scope, meaning the original brief, every later directive, and the prior agent's report and branch. This holds for a fix round, a follow-up, a retry, and the next queue item. Resume, message, or queue a follow-up on an existing subagent only when the new work strictly needs state that lives in that agent and is costly to move: its local checkout, its uncommitted changes, or a process it still runs, such as a dev server, a simulator, or a babysit watcher. A stop or hold order to a running agent is not reuse. A role such as a PR owner outlives its agent. Once that agent returns, a fresh agent takes the role's next round. Interrupt-chained resumes silently drop directives, so fire a fresh subagent with consolidated scope rather than trusting a \\\"done\\\" summary.\\n\\n## Throughput checkpoint\\n\\nFor nontrivial multi-step work, write these four todo items before fan-out, as required by Feature step 3. Keep an inapplicable dimension with n/a and a reason. Rewrite the checkpoint at phase boundaries and return it with the evidence.\\n\\n- Blocking first steps. Gates run before fan-out.\\n- Independent workstreams. Disjoint files, services or layers parallelize. Shared writes serialize.\\n- Shared mutable state. Default to splitting the target using the separate-before-serializing-shared-state principle leaf. Serialize only for real invariants.\\n- Smallest safe decomposition. If one worker is best, name why.\\n\\nCode-coupled work goes to one owner with the checkpoint inline. That owner fans out after the blocking phase when authorized and supported. Parent-level fan-out is for independent artifacts. Assignment records and bookkeeping do not prove workers ran. Keep decision trails in an explicit owned local output path per show-me-your-work and its log helper contract. Missing helper setup or persistence stays visible; do not invent saved logs or cross-session continuity.\\n\\n## Integrated helper and delivery bindings\\n\\nRead the selected playbook's complete adapted helper and prompt contracts before dependent execution.
