`<logfile> <phase> <decision> <why> <evidence> <result>`.\\n\\n  The nonempty logfile is an explicit owned local path.\\n\\n  Tabs, newlines and carriage returns are rejected before writes, and formula-leading cells are quoted.\\n\\n  Read back the trail and retain the skill's scoped transcript audit, cross-model review and Attention requirements.\\n\\n  Append-only logging proves neither unattended execution nor recovery.\\n- Multi-phase plans use [check-plan.mjs](scripts/check-plan.mjs) as `bun --no-install <package-root>/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/check-plan.mjs <plan.md>`.\\n\\n  It takes one positional path and reads without edits.\\n\\n  Exit 2 means usage or unreadable input, 1 means format problems, and 0 means structural success.\\n\\n  Keep capability declarations, live and perf evidence, independent review and exact-head receipts.\\n\\n  It checks pstack plans only.\\n\\n  It never validates another controller's plan, implementation or host behavior.\\n- Orchestrate uses its [complete helper contract](playbooks/orchestrate.md) and the real [CLI](scripts/orch/orch.ts) and [store](scripts/orch/store.ts).\\n\\n  Bind `bun --no-install `inbox drain --peek --json` and actual drain receipts with durable readback before classification. `ledger check <pr> <full-head-sha>` returns NOT-VERIFIED and exit 2 for a missing key.\\n\\n  Usage or runtime errors exit 1.\\n\\n  Ledger rows replace the same key without enforcing verifier precedence.\\n\\n  The caller owns precedence, append-only attempt history, exact SHA syntax, artifact inspection, terminal states, permissions and current-head filtering.\\n\\n  The helper never spawns, waits or wakes workers.\\n\\n  Stale locks require ownership review, and `--force` requires `--owner`, `--repo`, PR selection, `--head <number=full-40-character-head>` for every watched member, `--max-polls <remaining-count>` and `--timeout <remaining-seconds>`.\\n\\n  Freeze queued order with `--queued-stack --stack-prs <bottom-to-top-list>`.\\n\\n  Connected mode uses `--stack`, mutually exclusive with queued mode. `check` adds `--status-only`.\\n\\n  Inspect NDJSON schemaVersion 1 Missing create-skill, deslop, matching controls or independent review remains an explicit blocked gate even when a reduced local artifact is useful.\\n\\n## Writing the reply\\n\\nWrite the reply clean as you draft it. A cleanup pass after drafting does not remove these patterns.\\n\\n- **Short declarative sentences.** One thought per sentence, ended with a period.\\n- **No long-dash character anywhere.** Write a file-list bullet as a sentence (\\\"`main.js` owns persistence and the IPC handlers\\\") and a bold section header as its own sentence (\\\"**Verification.** End to end via CDP\\\").\\n- **A colon as a mid-sentence connector is also out** (unslop rule 14). A colon before a list is fine.\\n- **Terse is not an excuse to drop content.** Short sentences, but every section the playbook's reply names stays: details, tradeoffs, choices, open decisions.\\n- **Frame impact for the consumer and the maintainer.** Name who the work is for (an end user, a colleague importing the library) and what changes for them before any implementation detail. Then what the next engineer who owns this code inherits. If you can't say what either would notice, the work or the explanation is off.\\n- **Never fabricate a link, citation, or transcript reference.** Link only artifacts you produced or read this session.\\n- **Every claim carries its evidence or its label in the same sentence.** Measured, inferred, or guess. A prediction or an unseen cause is a guess. Never hand the human a check you could run.\\n\\nEvery playbook ends with a reply written this way. Link to the actual PR you read or created when authorized. Never claim a draft is a created PR. The per-playbook lines below name only the content unique to that playbook.\\n\\n## Comments\\n\\nComments follow the same rule as the reply. Write them clean as you go. Keep a comment only for a non-obvious *why* the code can't show. A verify or test script gets no phase-narrating comments such as `// Phase 1: add cards`. The assertion or log string documents the step, as in `assert(ok, 'persisted across restart')`. This applies to every file you produce, including the delegate's diff.\\n\\n## Playbooks\\n\\nOpen an available todo list, or an authorized local Markdown checklist if no todo operation exists. Record that reduced tool binding. Its first items are the matched playbook's steps, copied in verbatim, before any task-specific todos. A step you choose not to do stays in the list with a one-line `skip: <reason>`. Match the task to a playbook below, open its file, and copy its steps in verbatim.\\n\\nA large or cross-cutting effort (a migration across many call sites, an ambitious multi-part change), or work the user steps away from to trust later, routes to the **figure-it-out** skill even when a narrower playbook like Feature fits. Use **figure-it-out** whenever no bundled playbook fits. It designs a bespoke, rigorous playbook for the task. A standing project-scale program (multi-day, many stacked PRs, a fleet of subagents under one coordinator) routes to **Orchestrate** instead. figure-it-out designs one bespoke run, orchestrate runs the program.\\n\\n- **Investigation.** Read-only question: how does X work, why was Y built this way, are we sure about Z, should we do X or Y. `playbooks/investigation.md`.\\n- **Bug fix.** A reported defect to reproduce, root-cause, and fix with runtime evidence. `playbooks/bug-fix.md`.\\n- **Perf issue.** A measured slowness to trace and improve against a baseline. `playbooks/perf-issue.md`.\\n- **Hillclimb.** Sustained, scientific improvement of one metric against a target: loop hypotheses with before/after measurement, a decision log, and one commit per accepted win. Distinct from Perf issue, which is a one-off fix. `playbooks/hillclimb.md`.\\n- **Runtime forensics.** Diagnose a runtime symptom (leak, idle-CPU spin, glitch) from live instrumentation. The deliverable is a diagnosis, not a fix. `playbooks/runtime-forensics.md`.\\n- **Trace forensics.** Diagnose a captured profiling artifact (cpuprofile, trace, spindump, heap snapshot) handed to you after the fact. The deliverable is a diagnosis, not a fix. `playbooks/trace-forensics.md`.\\n- **Feature.** New or changed behavior, built from a named data shape. `playbooks/feature.md`.\\n- **Refactoring.** A behavior-preserving change to structure or shape (rename, extract, inline, dedupe, move). `playbooks/refactoring.md`.\\n- **Prototype.** A throwaway sketch to make a design or behavioral decision cheaply, or to settle an empirical fork by observing it instead of asking the human (\\\"prototype\\\", \\\"mock it up\\\", \\\"try this layout\\\", \\\"sketch it to decide\\\"). `playbooks/prototype.md`.\\n- **Visual parity.** Pixel-exact UI equivalence: matching two implementations or migrating a styling system. `playbooks/visual-parity.md`.\\n- **Authoring or modifying a skill.** Writing or editing a SKILL.md. `playbooks/authoring-a-skill.md`.\\n- **Eval.** Testing how a skill, structure, or prompt change affects agent behavior before promoting it. `playbooks/eval.md`.\\n- **Babysit.** Driving a PR or a stack to merge-ready: conflicts, review threads, CI. `playbooks/babysit.md`.\\n- **Shipping.** The half after Babysit. Independently verifying a green stack, then landing the contiguous verified run bottom-up through `gh` by default or Origin when its CLI is available. `playbooks/shipping.md`.\\n- **Autonomous run.** A long task to drive to completion without stopping (\\\"run until done\\\", \\\"/loop until X\\\"). `playbooks/autonomous-run.md`.\\n- **Orchestrate.** A standing project handed to one coordinator chat: multi-day, many stacked PRs, dozens to hundreds of subagents, minimal human turns (\\\"run this whole project\\\", \\\"own this migration until it lands\\\"). Distinct from Autonomous run, which drives one task to a predicate. Work one agent could finish inside the session's budget routes there, not here, however program-shaped the phrasing sounds. `playbooks/orchestrate.md`.\\n- **Autopilot-full.** A queue of independent PRs run to merged with full autonomy. One owner per PR carries build through merge, and the root swarm-verifies each PR before its owner merges (\\\"autopilot this queue\\\", \\\"full autopilot\\\", one-owner-per-PR programs). `playbooks/autopilot-full.md`.\\n- **Autopilot-stack.** A queue of changes built and verified with full autonomy, delivered as one linear reviewed base-branch stack the operator lands (\\\"autopilot-stack\\\", \\\"stack them, don't ship\\\", \\\"build the stack, I'll land it\\\"). `playbooks/autopilot-stack.md`.\\n- **Session pickup.** Resuming or taking over a prior agent's in-flight work from a transcript, cloud-agent URL, or pushed branch. `playbooks/session-pickup.md`.\\n- **Pause safely.** Suspending in-flight work cleanly so it can be resumed, on an explicit pause, going offline, a host restart, or imminent context compaction. The complement to Session pickup. Full steps: `playbooks/pause-safely.md`.\\n- **Multi-phase or multi-PR plan.** Work that spans phases or stacked PRs. `playbooks/multi-phase-plan.md`.\\n- **Worktree and simulator cleanup.** up space\\\", \\\"delete old simulators\\\"). `playbooks/worktree-cleanup.md`.\\n- **Opening a PR.** Invoked at the end of every other playbook. `playbooks/opening-a-pr.md`.\\n\"\n    }\n  },\n  {\n       "text": "# Boundary contract\n\nActual current code has a required object guard in adapter.ts, a cast of unvalidated quantity, and redundant nil/quantity guards in both internal functions. All callers are supplied. Quantity is a positive integer; one item costs 125 integer cents. Missing, null, zero, negative, fractional and string quantities return the exact invalid quantity response. Non-object payloads and malformed JSON retain their distinct existing errors.\n\nTarget: parse/narrow unknown raw values in the HTTP adapter before constructing an Order with required quantity:number. Internal priceOrder(Order) and subtotal(number) remain pure arithmetic and contain no duplicate missing-value/type/range guards after the boundary guarantees those invariants. Keep the necessary outer adapter guard and relocate quantity checks rather than deleting validation indiscriminately. Migrate internal-caller.ts to the final type. Preserve boundary.test.mjs behavior and replace or remove obsolete guard-only assertions in internal-guards.test.mjs as part of the authorized case, not preparation. There is no direct untrusted caller of the internal domain function in this complete private fixture.\n\nNo source has been fixed and no test has been run in preparation. Run strict static verification and Bun behavior tests during the actual case; distinguish source reading, runtime checking and type checking.\n"

      "text": "import { test, expect } from 'bun:test';\nimport { orderHttp } from './adapter.ts';\n\ntest('additional raw quantity variants reject at the boundary', () => {\n  for (const raw of ['{\"quantity\":false}', '{\"quantity\":true}', '{\"quantity\":[]}', '{\"quantity\":{}}', '{\"quantity\":1e400}']) {\n    expect(orderHttp(raw)).toEqual({ status: 400, body: { error: 'invalid quantity' } });\n  }\n});\n\ntest('scalar payloads retain the object error', () => {\n  for (const raw of ['0', 'true', 'false']) {\n    expect(orderHttp(raw)).toEqual({ status: 400, body: { error: 'invalid order' } });\n  }\n});\n\ntest('quantity one and unrelated fields preserve pricing', () => {\n  expect(orderHttp('{\"quantity\":1,\"extra\":\"ignored\"}')).toEqual({ status: 200, body: { total: 125 } });\n});\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/invalid-input/outputs/working/adapter.ts",
      "bytes": 675,
      "sha256": "bca23a43f2cd06e1e5975dbe3608885746736adbd3f90f3fea1171da477ce60a",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-invalid-input",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { priceOrder } from './domain';\nexport function orderHttp(raw: string): { status: number; body: { total?: number; error?: string } } {\n  let value: unknown;\n  try { value = JSON.parse(raw); } catch { return { status: 400, body: { error: 'invalid json' } }; }\n  if (typeof value !== 'object' || value === null || Array.isArray(value)) return { status: 400, body: { error: 'invalid order' } };\n  if (!('quantity' in value) || typeof value.quantity !== 'number' || !Number.isInteger(value.quantity) || value.quantity <= 0) return { status: 400, body: { error: 'invalid quantity' } };\n  return { status: 200, body: { total: priceOrder({ quantity: value.quantity }) } };\n}\n"
    },
    {

      "bytes": 189,

      "owner": "local-013-initial-invalid-input",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { priceOrder } from './domain';\nimport type { Order } from './domain';\nexport function exampleTotal(): number {\n  const order: Order = { quantity: 2 };\n  return priceOrder(order);\n}\n"

      "text": "import { test, expect } from 'bun:test';\nimport { orderHttp } from './adapter.ts';\nimport { exampleTotal } from './internal-caller.ts';\ntest('all external invalid quantities retain boundary error', () => {\n  for (const raw of ['{}','{\"quantity\":null}','{\"quantity\":0}','{\"quantity\":-1}','{\"quantity\":1.5}','{\"quantity\":\"2\"}']) {\n    expect(orderHttp(raw)).toEqual({ status: 400, body: { error: 'invalid quantity' } });\n  }\n});\ntest('existing outer guard and parser remain necessary', () => {\n  for (const raw of ['null','[]','\"text\"']) expect(orderHttp(raw)).toEqual({ status: 400, body: { error: 'invalid order' } });\n  expect(orderHttp('{')).toEqual({ status: 400, body: { error: 'invalid json' } });\n});\ntest('valid external and typed internal consumers', () => {\n  expect(orderHttp('{\"quantity\":2}')).toEqual({ status: 200, body: { total: 250 } });\n  expect(exampleTotal()).toBe(250);\n});\n"
